=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums of length n and a 2D array queries, where queries[i] = [l_i, r_i].
For each queries[i]:

Select a subset of indices within the range [l_i, r_i] in nums.
Decrement the values at the selected indices by 1.

A Zero Array is an array where all elements are equal to 0.
Return true if it is possible to transform nums into a Zero Array after processing all the queries sequentially, otherwise return false.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,0,1], queries = [[0,2]]
Output: true
Explanation:

For i = 0:

Select the subset of indices as [0, 2] and decrement the values at these indices by 1.
The array will become [0, 0, 0], which is a Zero Array.




Example 2:

Input: nums = [4,3,2,1], queries = [[1,3],[0,2]]
Output: false
Explanation:

For i = 0:

Select the subset of indices as [1, 2, 3] and decrement the values at these indices by 1.
The array will become [4, 2, 1, 0].


For i = 1:

Select the subset of indices as [0, 1, 2] and decrement the values at these indices by 1.
The array will become [3, 1, 0, 0], which is not a Zero Array.




 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
0 <= nums[i] <= 10^5
1 <= queries.length <= 10^5
queries[i].length == 2
0 <= l_i <= r_i < nums.length

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

class Solution:
    def minZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        def check(k):
            diff=[0]*(len(nums)+1)
            for l,r,val in queries[:k]:
                diff[l]+=val
                diff[r+1]-=val
            cur=0
            for i in range(len(nums)):
                cur+=diff[i]
                if cur < nums[i]: return False
            return True
...
```

Let's attempt formal proof:
Let c_i = number of queries j such that l_j <= i <= r_j.
Necessity: Each query can decrement nums[i] by at most 1. To reduce nums[i] to 0, need at least nums[i] queries covering i, so c_i >= nums[i].
Sufficiency: If c_i >= nums[i], for each index i choose any nums[i] of the c_i covering queries to include i. For each query j, let S_j be the set of indices i for which j was chosen. Then S_j is subset of [l_j,r_j]. Applying all queries with these subsets decrements each i exactly nums[i] times, resulting zero. This is valid because each query can select any subset. Thus condition.

Wait: If for each index i choose any nums[i] covering queries, a query j may have S_j containing many indices. That's allowed. So yes.

Now code:
```
from typing import List
class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        n = len(nums)
        diff = [0]*(n+1)
        for l,r in queries:
            diff[l] += 1
            diff[r+1] -= 1
        cur = 0
        for i in range(n):
            cur += diff[i]
            if cur < nums[i]:
                return False
        return True
```

```
class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        n = len(nums)
        diff = [0]*(n+1)
        for l,r in queries:
            diff[l]+=1
            diff[r+1]-=1
        for i in range(1,n):
            diff[i]+=diff[i-1]
        for i in range(n):
            if diff[i] < nums[i]: return False
        return True
```
Yes.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        n = len(nums)
        diff = [0] * (n + 1)
        for l, r in queries:
            diff[l] += 1
            diff[r + 1] -= 1
        
        cur = 0
        for i in range(n):
            cur += diff[i]
            if cur < nums[i]:
                return False
        return True
```

Let's maybe think about "commit-implement-recheck" and "Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block." The final answer can be:
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        n = len(nums)
        diff = [0] * (n + 1)
        for l, r in queries:
            diff[l] += 1
            diff[r + 1] -= 1

        cur = 0
        for i in range(n):
            cur += diff[i]
            if cur < nums[i]:
                return False
        return True
```
This is final.

=== ANSWER ===
should be final code only. Good.

Let's output code.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def isZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        n = len(nums)
        diff = [0] * (n + 1)

        for l, r in queries:
            diff[l] += 1
            diff[r + 1] -= 1

        cur = 0
        for i in range(n):
            cur += diff[i]
            if cur < nums[i]:
                return False

        return True
```
